On Friday she said she delivered a letter to Fidan and the Turkish energy minister from Energy Minister Tim Hodgson expressing Canada 's desire to co-operate with Turkey on nuclear power . <p> " Canada is a global leader in nuclear technology and we see significant opportunities to work together to advance not only energy security , but also economic growth and clean energy development , " she said . <p> Anand said Canada has been pitching Candu reactors to Turkish officials but the discussions are still " in early days . " <p> " The visit to Darlington resulted following a conversation that Minister Fidan and I have had over the course of a number of months , " Anand told reporters . <p> " Turkey has interest in the ( small modular reactor ) technology . It has interest in the nuclear sector . Canada is a leader in these sectors . @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ one of them , in our conversations with our international counterparts . And this a conversation that is beginning and we 're very excited . " <p> The federal government unveiled its nuclear strategy last week . <p> The plan looks to sell more Candu reactors around the world with a goal of entering at least four new international markets by 2040 . It also seeks to " engage six to 10 new nuclear entrant markets over a 15-year horizon , cementing Canada as their partner of choice . " <p> The push to sell Candu to new markets is an attempt to establish Canada as a reliable uranium export partner and to use nuclear energy as a geopolitical lever . <p> Fidan said that while his country is " specifically focusing on green energy , " such as hydroelectric dams , that wo n't be enough to meet the country 's energy needs . <p> " Nuclear energy is something that we are in need of , " Fidan said through an interpreter , adding he also met with officials from Ontario Power Generation , which owns the Darlington @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ gave me an opportunity to see the specific technology in Canada , " he said . " And of course , one of the issues that affected our attention is that this reactor is working without enriched uranium . " <p> Turkey is Canada 's military ally by virtue of its NATO membership and Fidan has echoed the Canadian government 's argument that Europeans should complement the military alliance without duplicating the work of NATO . <p> Prime Minister Mark Carney has sought to build stronger relations with numerous countries other than the United States . Anand visited Ankara in March and said Canada is focused on four pillars of its relationship with Turkey : energy exports , economic opportunities and trade , defence and security , and people-to-people ties . <p> During that visit , Fidan said in Turkish that relations with Canada should be elevated " to a strategic level " to pursue mutual interests and global peace . <p> Anand told The Canadian Press in March that both countries were sorting out how to frame their ties . <p> " The ultimate nomenclature that is used to @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ , but there 's no question that we share a vision for an elevated bilateral relationship , " she said . <p> Global Affairs Canada 's online profile of Turkey calls it " an emerging market of broad interest to Canada , " with opportunities for Canadian companies in sectors such as agriculture , life sciences , mining and infrastructure . <p> Turkey has helped Ottawa evacuate Canadians from various Middle East crises and bring home activists detained by Israel for joining flotillas that sought to bring aid to Palestinians in Gaza . <p> The bilateral relationship has experienced tensions , especially over Canada 's large Armenian diaspora . <p> Canada has long held that Turkey committed a genocide against Armenians a century ago , a claim that Ankara has repeatedly denied . <p> Ottawa also suspended arms sales to Turkey seven years ago -- and tightened those rules two years later , before dropping them -- in response to reports that Turkey was diverting Canadian military components to Azerbaijan for use against Armenia and ethnic Armenians . <p> Turkey has criticized Canada for granting refugee status to political dissidents @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ Journalists says Turkey is among the top jailers of journalists under the country 's terrorism and criminal codes . 
@@138651006 <p> Justin Ling is a staff columnist for the Star and an investigative journalist based in Montreal . Reach him by email : jling@thestar.ca <p> Israel 's right to defend itself " does not grant us the right to oppress others , " the Israeli newspaper Haaretz once warned in an editorial . Seizing new land requires ruling that land , and rule without representationbrings resistance . In turn , it continued , " resistance brings in its wake oppression . Oppression brings in its wake terrorism and counterterrorism . " <p> Published after the Six-Day War in 1967 , it was as true then as it is now . The dire situation Haaretz predicted has , in fact , come to pass . Israel 's willingness to oppress Palestiniansand neighbouring states has become not onlyintolerable but alsodangerous to its own survival . <p> Fraught as the situationmay be , it 's time Canada severed normal diplomatic ties with Israel -- bringing any other willing democracies along with us -- until Israel abides by international law and liberal norms . Palestinian self-determination , as well as long-term peace in the @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ Withdrawing our ambassador fromTel Aviv would be a powerful symbol of Canada 's rejection of the policies of Israeli Prime Minister Benjamin Netanyahu . Recalling an ambassador is a clear sign that normal relations between two countries have become untenable ; it is meant to isolate bad actors from the global diplomatic system , which itself is meant to prioritize peace and co-operation . <p> In this case , it would also signal that Canada sides with thehundreds of thousands of Israeli citizens who have been protesting their government'scorruption , warmongering and democratic backsliding . <p> Meanwhile , Israeli aggression is extending along other fronts . In the West Bank , extremist settlers have been mounting violent attacks against the Palestinians who live there . A report this year from the United Nations High Commissioner for Human Rights foundthat 36,000 Palestinians have been displaced from their homes in the West Bank -- " a scale previously unseen . " This violence has been " coordinated , strategic and largely unchallenged , " it noted , adding that Israeli authorities have been " directing , participatingin or enabling this conduct . @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ after years of autocratic rule and civil war , Israel has launched airstrikes and sent its forces deeper into the Golan Heights . In Lebanon , ithas killed thousands of peoplein the name of fighting Hezbollah . And after decades of trying , Netanyahu finally convinced the United States to partner with Israel on a disastrous war in Iran . Despite a ceasefire , Israel 's continued bombardment of Lebanon has persistently derailed peace talks . <p> All told , Israel has seized more than 600 square kilometres of Lebanon , as well as nearly 200square kilometres each of Gaza and Syria . Along with the expansion of illegal settlements in the West Bank , itadds up to roughly 1,000 square kilometresofterritory that Israel now controls and may occupyindefinitely . <p> The IDF hasperpetrated systemic human-rights abuses inside Israel 's military prisons , according to whistleblowers within the armed forces . Efforts to investigate these allegations have been stymiedbecause such abuses have become state policy . Amid all this , Netanyahu has diminishedthe independent oversight function of the courts and launchedan intimidation campaign against the free press . <p> Israel @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ founding principles . It has rationalized these violationsby insisting it is surrounded by existential threats -- but this justification has become absurd . Much has changed since the Six-Day War . Virtually all of Israel 's neighbours , from Syria to Lebanon tothe Gulf States , claim to want better ties with Tel Aviv . <p> The primary threat to Israel comes from extremism within the Palestinian populations to whom it long ago promised sovereignty . Israelis themselves have recognized that frustrating Palestinian self-determination only breeds more extremism . Yet even before October 7 , 2023 , Netanyahu 's government worked furiously to dash hopes of a two-state solution.Thanks to reporting from Israeli news outlets , we knowNetanyahu even directly supported Hamas in a bid to undermine the established Palestinian Authority . <p> The wayforward is n't to turn the clock back a century and imagine a world without Israel . Rather , it 's to stick tothe foundational precepts of Zionism , before they were perverted by Netanyahu . <p> It was then-prime minister Yitzhak Rabin who in 1995 recognized building a homeland for the Jewish people had meant @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ is not a peaceful solution , " he told the Knesset then . <p> His plan for peace , which promised Palestinians theirsovereignty , wasthe ambitious Oslo Accords , negotiated with help from the West . These were viciously opposed back then -- by Hamas in Gaza , and by Netanyahu in Israel . Today , we bear witness to a world in which both opposing parties got their way . <p> Only a total ceasefire , managed by new leaders , can offer a path toward peace . <p> As Susie Linfield argues in her book " The Lion 's Den , " " it is up to Israel to save itself . " Published in 2019 , the book warns that rejecting peace and allowing Netanyahu 's paranoia and fear to grow is a dead end . " Ifthat democratic revival does fail -- which , in my view , means the suicide ofZionism -- Israelis will have only themselves to blame , " Linfield writes . <p> Protests againstNetanyahu , including by families of the October 7 victims and hostages , have roiled Israel for years . @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ continuing to prosecute a war that seems to have nopurpose beyond prolonging Netanyahu 's political career . Under one-third of Israelis want Netanyahu as their leader , and the prime minister 's Likud party is on track for a historically bad result . <p> The men most likely to replace Netanyahu are n't exactly peaceniks . One is the centrist Gadi Eisenkot , a hawkish former IDF general ; the other isthe ultra-conservative Naftali Bennett , who has run to the right even of Netanyahu on Palestine . Either candidate wouldalmost certainly have to form a government supported by leftists , centrists and assorted critics of the war -- perhaps evenArab-Israeli parties . If nothing else , this would steerIsrael back toward political pluralism . <p> While Canada should not meddle in Israel 's elections , we can and should make it clear that we willnot continue to partner with Israel if it maintainsNetanyahu 's policies of destruction and death.Our allyship , in other words , is not absolute or unconditional , nor should it be for any country . <p> Last year , Canada and some of our alliesgingerlytook @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ Israeli cabinet ministers who had incited and then cheered on settler violence.We have been too afraid to go further -- but it 's now or never . <p> And Canada has more leverage than Spain . We can and should suspend the Canada-Israel free trade agreement and pause the various mechanisms of co-operation that were once a sourceof pride for both countries . <p> Israel , of course , wouldlevy accusations of antisemitism against Canada -- as it has against Spain -- ifwe were to do this . But theseactionsare justified and not entirely without precedent . <p> Generally , Canada has preferred to maintain its diplomatic ties , even with illiberal nations , opting to lobby rather than denounce . But sometimes intransigence has pushed us to make a statement , as we did in the case of Iran in 2012 and Belarus in 2021 . We withdrew our ambassadors then -- not because we wanted to destroy those states , but because we believed drastic measures were requiredto effect political change within them . <p> Recognizing Israel 's right to exist means holding Israelaccountable for its actions , @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ relationship with Israel -- which Canada has , at times , been even more enthusiastic about than the U.S. -- gives us the moral legitimacy to make ourpoint clearly . And despite what he would have you believe , Netanyahu is sensitive to this sort of criticism . That 's why he lashes out so forcefully at even the most tepid of condemnations . <p> There are just five months untilIsraeli voters head to the polls . Before then , Canada should make it clear that our commitment to the existence of a Jewish state does not mean we are prepared totolerate the crimes its current government is committing . <p> Opinion Headlines Newsletter <p> Take a stand with this regular roundup of the best from our columnists . <p> Error ! @@138679846 <h> Canada 's World Cup dream ends with a loss to Morocco and a haunting feeling of what could have been <p> Canadian players dejected on the field after their World Cup run ends with loss to Morocco . <p> Eric Smith/The Associated Press <p> HOUSTON -- What does it sound like when a dream dies ? It sounds like Casablanca , or Rabat , maybe Marrakesh . <p> Rampant Moroccan joy and raucous song filled Houston 's cavernous cooling centre in the moments after referee Michael Oliver sounded the World Cup 's most overworked whistle for a final time . The banks of ruby red at the southern end of the stadium chanted as they checked Google Flights for options to Boston where a World Cup quarterfinal awaits . <p> At the opposite end , Canada coach Jesse Marsch pulled players and staff into one last huddle , their summer odyssey now over . This time , Marsch asked TV crews to move away . But when pushed post-match as to what the thrust of the message was , when the cameras had shown him squeezing a thumb and index finger close together , he was happy to share . <p> " If I remember correctly , I was saying , the smallest details make a difference when you 're on top of the match , " he said . " The ability to be the one that makes those plays and is on top of those details is the difference . " <p> The final scoreline in the opening encounter of the last 16 was 3-0 . It was an altogether harsh reflection on what had played out here for a couple of hours . A harsh endnote too for a tournament where a miserly defensive defiance has been Canada 's calling card . <p> Goals two and three only arrive because Marsch 's team was plunged into such a perilous position when the clocks here hit 49:16 . Ultimately that is what will be recorded as the time of death , a Canadian summer reverie perishing in an instant . The real killer , the thing which will haunt on through days and weeks to come , was how easily Canada succumbed , how it handed the weapon to a cold-blooded opponent . <p> After playing a key part in what was surely the best half of football Canada has played under Marsch , 20-year-old Luc de Fougerolles re-emerged with his teammates and set about more of the same . But when a ball was played down the right side of Canada 's box , he clattered into Soufiane Rahimi , a rash moment which laid the ground for the decider . <p> Captain Achraf Hakimi stood over the free kick and Canadian bodies in ice black flooded into their own box . Moroccans left them at it , just a handful joining the fray . What we were left with was all 10 Canadian outfielders within 12 yards of goalkeeper Maxime Cr ? peau marking just five opponents . Jonathan David stepped off to the side of a one-man wall to offer cover for a pull-back but it was n't nearly enough . Hakimi duly angled a pass perfectly back to the edge of the box where Azzedine Ounahi side-footed it through a sea of legs and beyond Cr ? peau . <p> Just 5 Moroccans inside the box but all 10 Canadian outfielders camped within 12 yards of Max Crepeau 's goal . Surely Jonathan David had to drop out further and he could have easily have had another body go with him . A well-worked routine but all in all , an awful Canada switch-off **26;33;TOOLONG <p> The smallest details . Schoolboy details , really . The lack of awareness and anticipation was exacerbated by David 's half-stretch to intercept . <p> " It 's a cleverly worked set-piece which catches us out , " said Alistair Johnston . " It 's a frustrating one because it 's such a cheap goal to give up . " <p> Cheap but so costly . Morocco had had just a solitary touch in Cr ? peau 's box in the dominant first half . Canada did n't upset an opponent and the debate as to whether Marsch has an emphatic , statement win on his watch may persist . Its best performance came here , in its heaviest defeat . This remains a funny old game . <p> David was closest to the free kick which ended it all and never got close enough to eliminate the threat . Replays suggest that stretching a few more sinews may have got him there . " <p> Read more <p> Updated 9 hrs ago <h> How the FIFA World Cup forced downtown Toronto to fix its gridlock problem -- for a few weeks <p> But experts say it will take more ambition and political will to make the FIFA experience the norm . <p> Despite fears that the surge of FIFA attendees would overwhelm the city , getting around Toronto has -- generally -- been smooth-sailing for commuters . It 's all gone according to a transit-heavy plan that city and provincial agencies have worked on for months after pressing the need for commuting options that do n't involve cars . <p> During FIFA World Cup match days , there were fewer drivers on the road , but those who chose to drive faced longer trips due to congestion . <p> Meanwhile , more commuters opted to take the TTC or used the city 's bikeshare system to get around . <p> " This is a big win for Toronto transportation , " said Matti Siemiatycki , director of U of T 's Infrastructure Institute . But , he added , there 's more work that needs to be done if Toronto wants to continue , as it has pushed for years , to move more commuters off the roads and toward car-free alternatives . <h> TTC , bike share helped FIFA travellers <p> On the first three game days on June 12 , 17 and 20 , motor vehicle volumes on major expressways were between 10 and 30 per cent lower than normal , according to the city . Citywide , there was no clear pattern of drive times increasing , but in the core they were up by between about four and 24 per cent . Toronto officials attributed that to heavy traffic near the stadium ; roads around the match venue and Fan Festival watch party at Fort York and the Bentway were shut down during the tournament . <p> Meanwhile , a significant number of fans appeared to be getting to the games by walking , biking or transit . <p> The city said that factors such as kickoff time , traffic collisions and weather could also influence how attendees travelled during match days . <p> The Bike Share system , however , showed a significant uptick in ridership on match days , continuing the system 's meteoric rise in popularity . For all match days except for June 17 , Bike Share ridership was estimated to be at least 20 per cent higher than last year . On June 13 , the day after Canada 's opening match against Bosnia and Herzegovina and on the second day of the Taste of Little Italy street festival , Bike Share Toronto broke its single day trip record with 49,048 trips . <p> On the TTC , ridership across the five main corridors serving the World Cup ( buses and streetcars on Bathurst , Dufferin , King , Harbourfront and Ossington ) saw a combined 40 per cent jump in ridership compared to boardings last year . For the TTC , which has been struggling to recoup ridership , that boost has been significant . <p> " We know it 's not job done by any means , but these numbers show our preparations to deliver a good customer experience ( service , staffing , way-finding ) is focused in the right areas , " said Stuart Green , spokesperson for the TTC . <p> Andrew Posluns , Toronto 's traffic " czar , " said in a statement that the figures show the city 's World Cup transportation plan , which emphasized car-free transit modes , has worked . <p> " We are seeing greater use of public transit , cycling and walking , and vehicle travel times that , while higher in the downtown , remain consistent across the city as a whole , " said Posluns , whose official title is chief congestion officer . <p> He said that " even as we host this major event , Torontonians continue to move in and around the city . " <h> Toronto 's new normal ? <p> Ahead of the tournament the offices , restaurants and bars in the neighbourhood were cautiously optimistic they could handle any increased traffic , said Chris Chreston , director of communications at the Downtown West Business Improvement Area . <p> The organization represents 3,000 businesses in the busy part of the core between University Avenue and Bathurst Street , south of Queen Street , near World Cup sites . The coverage area includes the Entertainment District and King West Village , where establishments are familiar with congestion or road closures from games or concerts at the Rogers Centre and Scotiabank Arena . The soccer tournament did n't appear to cause significantly worse gridlock than those events . <p> " We would be the first to hear if there were a lot of complaints , and we have n't heard complaints , traffic-wise , " Chreston said . The organization 's takeaway is that " game day traffic has not been a hindrance to anyone 's operation . " <p> He attributed that at least in part to people heeding pleas from the city to avoid driving into downtown during the tournament . <p> " That message -- walk and take transit -- was received , and that 's lightened things up relatively for the people who have to ( drive ) and do n't have a choice , " he said . <p> " Strong coordination between governments , transit agencies and event partners has demonstrated that , with thoughtful planning and execution , Toronto can move people efficiently while minimizing disruption , " said TRBOT president and CEO Giles Gherson in a statement . ( Gherson was editor-in-chief of the Toronto Star from 2004 - 2006 . ) <p> He said the board hopes the city will take lessons it learned from the event to create " a more reliable transportation network " long after the games leave town . <p> Restricting traffic in the downtown core during FIFA boosted the city 's ability to ease congestion , Siemiatycki said . Downtown construction , especially lane closures , is the main reason for Toronto 's clogged streets , experts have told the Star in the past . The city also crammed much of its construction work on the TTC and on highways in the weeks leading up to FIFA , causing short-term headaches to ensure a smooth-running system during the matches . <p> Still , Siemiatycki added , while it might not be possible for the city to pause construction and treat every day like a FIFA match day , he wants to see Toronto to continue to be " ambitious " when it comes to transportation planning in the city , which FIFA proved is possible . <p> Read more <p> ARTICLE CONTINUES BELOW <p> ARTICLE CONTINUES BELOW <p> Updated 3 mins ago <h> Canadian fans say they are proud of men 's soccer team despite loss to Morocco <p> By Wolfgang Depner The Canadian Press <p> Canadian fans celebrate after Canada defeated South Africa during a World Cup round of 32 soccer match , at FIFA Fan Fest in Vancouver , on Sunday , June 28 , 2026 . Time to hang tough . <p> Read more <p> Updated July 3 , 2026 at 4:11 p.m . <h> This high-tech ball was involved in one of the most dramatic moments in World Cup history <p> ATLANTA ( AP ) -- It is called connected ball technology . And it was responsible for one of the most dramatic climaxes to a World Cup match ever . <p> It canceled Croatia 's late equalizer deep in added time against Portugal by detecting a touch that was undetectable to the naked eye and even video replays late Thursday . <p> Portugal won 2-1 in Toronto and advanced to the round of 16 , leaving Croatia players and fans devastated in the belief Josko Gvardiol 's goal was wrongly called offside by the VAR and referee Espen Esk ? s . <p> FIFA is relying on a high-tech soccer ball fitted with " advanced sensors " and insisted it got it right when determining Croatia 's Igor Mantanovic got the slightest of glances with his head , meaning Mario Palasic was in an offside position during the buildup to the goal . <p> The in-ball sensors were so finely tuned , FIFA said , they were " capable of determining any slight contact ... allowing officials an unprecedented level of data to make fast , accurate decisions . " <p> Here 's the science bit <p> The official " Trionda " World Cup ball , manufactured by Adidas , is fitted with a " small inertial measurement unit ( IMU ) sensor , " which FIFA says operates at around 500Hz and captures data 500 times per second . <p> FIFA says it can track ball acceleration and granular movements in three dimensions and can detect the exact moment a player makes contact . <p> The ball technology is combined with in-stadium cameras for tracking data that is transmitted in real time to video assistants . <p> As well as helping to determine offsides , the touch data can also be used for incidents such as handballs and penalties . <p> How reliable is it ? <p> The reason the call was so contentious was because even slow motion replays from numerous angles were visually inconclusive that Mantanovic made contact with Ivan Perisic 's in-swinging cross . <p> That is where reliance on the technology came in . Referee Esk ? s was instructed by the VAR to review the sideline monitor . Replays showed what FIFA calls a " heartbeat graphic " to indicate the moment the ball was touched and there was a clear spike as it apparently grazed Mantanovic 's head . <p> " No matter how fast the ball is moving or the spin of the ball , you can track it really effectively , " The AP was told by professor Manos Tentzeris from Georgia Tech 's school of electrical and computer engineering . " The position of the ball is 99.99% accurate ... you know exactly where the players are , even the tip of a shoe , which sometimes determines if someone is offside or onside . " <p> FIFA also used connected ball technology at the 2022 World Cup , and it was deployed at the most recent men 's European Championship in 2024 . <p> Tests were carried out from 2020-22 and the technology trialed at tournaments such as the Arab Cup and the Club World Cup . <p> This is not the first time <p> The ball sensors also had a decisive impact at Euro 2024 by detecting Denmark 's Joachim Andersen handled in the box against host Germany . After a VAR review a penalty was awarded and Kai Havertz scored and Germany won 2-0 . <p> At the Wheat Sheaf Tavern the other day , an extraordinary thing happened . It made me think seriously about beauty and soccer . Now , the Wheat Sheaf is Toronto 's oldest tavern , been going since 1849 , and many amazing things have no doubt happened there . This particular event might not be up at the top , but hear me out . <p> It was mid-afternoon . A bunch of patrons had watched France beat Sweden 3-0 in this World Cup . Two goals for Kylian Mbapp ? which meant he was level with Lionel Messi in this World Cup 's goal-scoring charts . Plus , Bradley Barcola scored in a dazzling performance by France . <p> Lots to talk about then . Discussion and banter were well under way . Then the door opened and a group walked in . <p> A brief silence descended as we stared at the newcomers . A group of eight , and they were Blue Jays fans . All in their Jays shirts , it looked like a family of six plus two others . They took over an empty booth and pulled in a nearby table . <p> It 's a bit rude to stare , but some people were mesmerized by the sight . During a World Cup , one co-hosted by Canada and in a city where tournament games are happening , it 's easy to forget that other events actually unfold . <p> Me , I 'm looking at the World Cup now with a soundtrack attached . One song and one song only . That 's the Tragically Hip 's " Ahead by a Century " ... <h> When Blue Jays fans walked into a World Cup bar <p> Some of us in the Wheat Sheaf were utterly oblivious to the fact that the Jays season was ongoing and there were sports fans attending baseball who were , in turn , oblivious to the World Cup . It looked like a secessionist movement had decided to invade the bar and set up camp . A provocation of sorts . <p> Eventually , an emissary was sent to open discussions . Well , in truth , it was a guy going to the bathroom who stopped on the way back to chat up the Jays fans . As in , " Hey , who are the Jays playing today ? " <p> He reported back . Yep , the Jays were playing a home game against the New York Mets at the Rogers Centre in a few hours . An attraction of this particular game was that it was a Loonie Dogs Night , meaning fans were able to purchase $1 hot dogs . It was suggested to the emissary that the one-dollar hot dogs should be tried at World Cup games to make the events more family friendly and fun , and the emissary was told that baseball is the beautiful game , not soccer . Oh , how we chuckled at that . <p> After some criticism of his outspoken manner earlier in the World Cup , Marsch has , if anything , doubled down . <h> The world was n't impressed with Canada vs . South Africa <p> But it made me think : Where , oh where , is the beautiful game at the World Cup ? You know , the flowing games with pretty moves , the balletic configuration of bodies , the player dancing with the ball in a way that 's visually pleasing and seems to defy the laws of physics . The tempo and virtuosity that takes your breath away . <p> If we 're looking for that type of elegant play , best not start with Canada . Tough to hear , I know , but there are limits to patriotism . Last Sunday 's match between Canada and South Africa was the only World Cup match played that day and drew a huge international audience and attention . It ended with last-minute ecstasy for Canada supporters . <p> The world saw it differently . The concluding remark from a man doing minute-by-minute online commentary for RTE ( national TV and radio in Ireland ) was this bit of derision : " Potentially one of the most tedious games of football ever played in a World Cup , and it is settled at the death by ( Stephen ) Eust ? quio . " A prolific English pundit posting online declared : " Absolutely terrible game . Possibly the worst World Cup game ever . " There was a lot of that to be found online and , fair enough really . It was excruciating to watch . <p> The beautiful game -- a term popularized by the Brazilian great Pel ? , who called it " o jogo bonito " in Portuguese -- is being played by France and its three best players : Mbapp ? , Michael Olise and Ousmane Demb ? l ? . Together they create a gorgeous spectacle of motion , timing and elegance . Spain has played with flashes of joyful grace , that short-passing link-up play that is enormously hard to master . Messi does it too , in the short passages of time when he truly participates for Argentina . You can only be awed by his understanding of space on the field and the way he accelerates , slows , accelerates again and turns with an unerringly accurate pass to a teammate . <p> The last of six World Cup games in Toronto was the craziest and the best , considering the stakes <p> So far then , we have n't really experienced all-out beauty played by an entire team . But beauty is where you choose to find it , and in a 48-team World Cup there 's plenty of charm and grace . There 's beauty in Cape Verde storming out of the early rounds to face Argentina in a knockout game and that particular beauty is not just in the fairy-tale narrative , but in the Cape Verde team played attacking soccer , scoring goals and enjoyed it all . There was beauty in the pulsating second half of Portugal 's victory over Croatia . Such panache in the passion of both sides . <p> I can not find baseball beautiful , but I do n't begrudge anyone the pleasure of it . I was seduced long ago by the rhythms and movements of soccer , and seduction is the key here . To believe in the ideal of soccer as the beautiful game is to be seduced into a noble concept : that soccer is n't a competition , but an occasion for the sublime to emerge , for outrageous creativity to burst forth . <p> We could do with more of that , and maybe it is coming in the torrid , intense final stages . Let 's hope so and , while we 're here , go Jays go . <p> Read more <p> ARTICLE CONTINUES BELOW <p> ARTICLE CONTINUES BELOW <p> Updated July 3 , 2026 at 4:11 p.m . <h> Puppy love as dog walker turns his pack of 13 into Argentina jersey-clad World Cup followers <p> BUENOS AIRES , Argentina ( AP ) -- Thirteen Argentina followers are roaming the streets of Buenos Aires proudly sporting their team 's jersey -- but they 're not on two legs , they 're on all fours , letting out the occasional bark . <p> As Argentina rides World Cup fever and dreams of beating Cape Verde in the round of 32 on Friday , a dog walker has added his pack to the local fanbase . <p> Nahuel Meneghini , 33 , walks the streets of the capital 's city center with the dogs wearing the team 's jerseys , leashes and collars in support of Lionel Messi ' s squad . <p> " I did it out of love for them and for Argentina . And for the World Cup , " said Meneghini -- known as " Nano " -- while speaking to The Associated Press on Thursday during a walk with the canine group . <p> The dog walker , also dressed head-to-toe like a fan , skilfully managed all the leashes , guiding the dogs along the narrow sidewalks of the capital 's San Crist ? bal neighborhood . <p> As they passed , smiles lit up the faces of onlookers who pulled out their phones to snap pictures . " Let 's go , Argentina ! " a woman shouted . <p> " These are the only joys we have because nothing ever goes our way , not for workers or retirees . Everything is against us , " said 80-year-old Edgardo P ? rez , who was taking photos of the group known on social media as " La Perroneta . " <p> The name alludes to " La Scaloneta , " the nickname of the national team aiming to retain its title at the World Cup under coach Lionel Scaloni . <p> Scaloni is admired in Argentina following the team 's triumphs at the World Cup and the Finalissima in 2022 , as well as the 2021 Copa Am ? rica . <p> Meneghini developed the idea of dressing the dogs in Argentina colors last week as it advanced from the group stage . <p> Two of the dogs he regularly walks , Sirio and Roberta , were wearing jerseys featuring Messi 's No. 10 . Their owners bought them after catching World Cup fever . <p> Meneghini loved the idea and decided to dress the rest of the dogs the same way . He stopped by a pet store and picked up jerseys in various sizes . The owners readily accepted the outfits . <p> The same went for the collars and leashes he crafted in light blue and white . He added three rivets to the leashes -- one for each of Argentina 's World Cup titles in 1978 , 1986 and 2022 -- while hanging tags reading " Los perros de Nano " ( Nano 's Dogs ) from the collars . <p> Meneghini offers the pet owners a good price for all the items . Other people in the neighborhood have started placing orders for their own pets . <p> The dogs and their walker cover around 60 blocks almost every day and have become local celebrities and social media stars . <p> " Now that is patriotism , " the 73-year-old Dora Maisano said as she watched the group pass by . " Not just peeing and pooping . Everyone wearing the No. 10 . Well done , congratulations -- they look so cute ! " <p> The pack will continue to wear the national colors " forever , " even if Argentina is eliminated from the competition , Meneghini said . " But I have faith that we 'll win a fourth cup . After the fourth one , I 'll add another rivet to the leashes . " <p> HOUSTON -- Ally McCoist is one of the more beloved TV analysts in British football . His thick Glasgow accent is made for sidekick storytelling duty . It 's augmented with a child-like wonder and glee when moments of beauty and calamity arise . <p> McCoist was on World Cup duty for British broadcaster ITV for Scotland 's second match of Group C June 19 , a meeting with Morocco in Boston . From the first minute , Scotland 's Tartan Army of supporters booed lustily whenever Morocco 's Achraf Hakimi got on the ball . Things reached a crescendo in the 20th minute when Hakimi stopped a Scottish breakaway with a cynical found . <p> " Not sure what Hakimi has done to upset the locals , " wondered McCoist into his ITV microphone . " But he 's done something . " <p> Hakimi is Morocco 's captain , the fearless leader of a rampant generation of Atlas Lions . He has excelled for giant clubs across four of Europe 's ' big five ' leagues -- Real Madrid , Borussia Dortmund , Inter Milan and Paris Saint-Germain -- helping the French powerhouse to back-to-back Champions League triumphs . In a football era when any club or country is only as good as its fullbacks , Hakimi is the game 's best . A quite phenomenal player . <p> The upset ' locals ' McCoist wondered about were mostly Scottish but neutrals too who had spent time perusing news headlines on the long trek out to Gillette Stadium . <p> World Cup run feels like a turning point and great football nations chant and sing . Canada , broadly , does not . Or at least , not enough . <h> A rape trial ordered -- and a captain who kept playing <p> Mere hours before kickoff , an appeals court in France ordered that Hakimi be formally charged with rape and , later this year , stand trial in relation to preliminary charges first issued more than three years ago . In March 2023 , a then 24-year-old woman told police she was raped by Hakimi at his home in a Paris suburb . <p> Hakimi has consistently denied any wrongdoing and spent the intervening time fighting to have the case dismissed . He has also continued to play for club and country , racking up 12 major trophies and earning an estimated $97 million in salary . Yet the Versailles appeals court said June 19 , according to AP , that " investigations conducted during the inquiry and the judicial investigation led the investigating chamber to conclude there is sufficient evidence against the player to stand trial . " <p> A date has n't been set but is expected in the fall . In a post on X , Hakimi said he 'd been " an easy target ... Justice looked me in the eye and told me : ' If you were not famous , there would never have been a case ' . " <p> On Thursday here , Hakimi took a full part in training with teammates at Houston Dynamo 's home . On Saturday in the giant NRG Stadium in the city 's south he will lead out Morocco to face Canada in the Last 16 of this World Cup . Jesse Marsch 's men of 2026 have already gone further than any Canadian side before . Hakimi and Morocco are the giant standing in the way of more history . <p> While Hakimi may feel that were he " not famous , there would never have been a case " , there is another argument : were he not famous , he may not have been able to continue working while facing trial for such a serious crime . And another : were he still famous but playing another sport or in another jurisdiction and time , he may have been immediately stood down from playing until justice has run its course . <p> After some criticism of his outspoken manner earlier in the World Cup , Marsch has , if anything , doubled down . <h> Hakimi , Mendes , Partey : a tournament 's uncomfortable truth <p> A deeply unsettling aspect of the next 48 hours of World Cup knockout action is that Hakimi is not alone . <p> Two other captains competing Friday are currently subject to rape charges or investigations . Cape Verde have been a tournament fairy tale yet its captain Ryan Mendes is being investigated by New Zealand police for allegedly raping a team liaison officer during a tournament there in March . Ghana skipper Thomas Partey , meanwhile , was denied entry into Canada for his side 's clash with Panama due to seven counts of rape he faces in the U.K. Both players insist they 're innocent . <p> While on-field outcomes are of a distant secondary importance in this context , there is an irony that were Canada facing Morocco in Vancouver , there is a possibility Hakimi would , like Partey , be barred from playing , a hammer blow to Morocco 's chances . <p> But they 're not . The sides will meet here in the deep red of Texas . If the exact legal mechanisms of such matters are complex , culturally at least , it does feel that the America of 2026 is now a place where having such charges hanging over you is n't as big an issue as elsewhere . Eighteen month ago , MMA fighter Conor McGregor was found liable of sexual assault in a civil trial in Ireland . Next week he returns to headline a UFC mega-event in Las Vegas and just days ago sat down for a grotesquely powder puff late night sit-down with Jimmy Fallon . It said so much about where America is at and yet should n't surprise when the Commander in Chief himself has also been found liable by a jury of sexual abuse . <p> For their part , FIFA responded to a query on the Mendes case by telling the New York Times that it " takes any allegation of misconduct extremely seriously . " <p> How seriously ? Yes , Houston might be as hot as the inside of a mouth full of jalape ? o poppers , and might be the ugliest major city in America and beyond , with gems so hidden you have to hunt . But the round of 16 at a World Cup is as good as it 's ever gotten for Canadian men 's soccer . They should savour every second . <p> And all that said , you can peek over the horizon , too . At 20 , Luc de Fougerolles is the youngest player on this World Cup roster , a fresh-faced 20-year-old who is built like a young bull and has started all but one of Canada 's games in the tournament . He gave way to Mo ? se Bombito in the round of 32 against South Africa ; de Fougerolles played the final 30 minutes plus stoppage time . Bombito is such an essential core piece for Canada , so you understood . Derek Cornelius staying in a starting role , less so . <p> But of all the discoveries of this tournament , de Fougerolles has become an unhidden gem , and the British-born Canadian centre back seems unsurprised about it , really . In the English vernacular , it 's like he was to the manor born . <p> " No , I knew I was ready , " de Fougerolles said Thursday . " In my mind , it 's always that I 'm going to start the game . So coming into it , I thought I was going to start , right ? Whether that was going to be or not to be , that was n't my decision . But I think the main thing ( is ) that I got prepared as if I 'm going to play , and then if I do n't play I 've got to be ready off the bench as well , if needed . " <p> De Fougerolles entered this tournament as a still-speculative piece . He started the third-place game at the 2024 Copa America against Uruguay and had 13 caps for Canada , but has spent his pro season on loan from Fulham at Dender , which finished last in Belgium 's top league and where he was 10th on his club in minutes played . True , Canada 's Promise David did talk him up before the tournament with gusto . And teammates do call him the Machine . <p> Luc de Fougerolles speaks to reporters during a June 10 Canada World Cup training session . <p> Sammy Kogan <p> " He 's 20 , he has so many caps already , he 's somebody that ( coach Jesse Marsch ) trusts in the back line without hesitation if somebody goes down , " David told reporters . " When we were playing against each other in Belgium , he was a really straightforward and serious guy for the 90 minutes , and whenever I 'm playing with him I 'm always careful about my well-being , because I do n't think Luc gives a f -- - about anybody 's Achilles or bones or anything . <p> For his part , de Fougerolles named Portuguese and Manchester City superstar R ? ben Dias as a player he admires and tries to emulate , and he does n't overthink things , he thinks . He just trusts his instincts , all the way . He might not start against Morocco on Saturday , though you could argue Cornelius has been the weaker of the two . <p> And in fairness , the 20-year-old has not been perfect . Switzerland was a learning experience . On the second Swiss goal , de Fougerolles had to chase a deep ball and just missed heading it back to Canadian goalkeeper Max Cr ? peau , then worked his way to the right side of hulking Swiss striker Breel Embolo , and then slipped . With Cornelius over to help , nobody was home on Johan Manzambi , and the Swiss scored what would stand as the winning goal . <p> " I think you see the standard is very high playing against the best players in the world , and I think maybe something I have n't had much of a kind of exposure to , " said de Fougerolles . " So Switzerland was a really good lesson , where you kind of see one moment will kind of lead to a goal , or something like that . <p> " But I think I love playing against the best players . It was a game I really enjoyed , the Switzerland game , playing against some of the best guys in the world . And then Morocco , similar , they 've got such good strikers , such good attackers . So I 'm looking forward to that a lot . " <p> It 's almost been forgotten already , but de Fougerolles also sent the brilliant ball to 22-year-old Nathan Saliba -- de Fougerolles called it an OK ball , maybe a little overcooked -- which Saliba juggled over to the 24-year-old David for Canada 's only goal against Switzerland . It was one of the most stunning goals of the tournament , and gave Canada a chance in that moment . But maybe it was one for the future , too . <p> Read more <p> ARTICLE CONTINUES BELOW <p> ARTICLE CONTINUES BELOW <p> Updated July 3 , 2026 at 4:11 p.m . <h> How coach Jesse Marsch is setting the tone for Canada 's World Cup dream run <p> Head coach Jesse Marsch celebrates after Canada 's late winning goal against South Africa in the World Cup round of 32 . <p> Andre Penner/The Associated Press <p> HOUSTON -- Jesse Marsch eventually started to sweat , but that was just the brain-baking Texas sun , probably . Canada 's coach has dragged his team to the final 16 at a World Cup , against a Morocco team that looks good . Or better than good . What do you think , Mr. Marsch ? <p> " I called preparing for Switzerland a horror show , right ? " said Marsch with a smile . " And if that 's what the case was , then preparing for Morocco is like a gory , horrible ... " <p> Nightmare ? It sounds like a nightmare . <p> " Yeah , it 's like I do n't want to watch them play , they 're too good , you know ? " said Marsch . " But we want to be here and we expect to be here , and so we know that everybody 's going to write us off . And in that 's an opportunity , right ? In the end , we are focused on trying to really be our best for this match and deliver the best performance of our lives , and that can give us a chance . " <p> Marsch is enjoying his time in the World Cup spotlight , and he should . Canada escaped its group , won in the round of 32 with a late Stephen Eust ? quio goal and now is facing a true global football giant for the first time in the tournament . <p> So , Canada will need to play careful football and fearless football here on Saturday , will have to be happy with this accomplishment but not satisfied , and clearly has to play its best match of the World Cup with more Mo ? se Bombito and Alphonso Davies than at any other point to have a chance . <p> But it starts with Marsch . After some criticism of his outspoken manner earlier in the tournament , Marsch has , if anything , doubled down . He used profanity more than once in his post-game press conference after defeating South Africa . There is clearly a sensitivity to criticism -- Marsch was criticized fairly viciously while managing Leeds -- and a determination to say it does n't bother him at all . <p> " I know that Americans , we get a certain rap for being boisterous , for being arrogant , for being outwardly vocal , and I know that in many ways that does describe me , or at least people love to describe me that way , " Marsch said Sunday . " But again , I do n't give a s -- t . All I care about is working with the teams I work with and the players I work with , and helping them be the best that they can be . " <p> And with Canada in what almost feels like a no-lose situation -- honestly , who expected this team ( injuries and all ) to reach the final 16 : outlasting Germany , the Netherlands , Japan , one of Portugal and Croatia , and South Korea ? -- Marsch appears to both be enjoying the moment and preparing for the next one in his inimitable style . <p> " I think we learned from the group phase , " Marsch said in Houston . " Say what you want about ( the 1-0 win over South Africa ) , should we have scored goals earlier ... we gave them nothing , right ? I mean , 0.14 ( expected ) goals against in a knockout phase ? Nothing . So people wanted to be critical ( and call it an ) ugly game . Then watch the next four games and they all went to extra time or penalties , or right at the end . Actually , we were the most dominant performance so far in the knockout round . <p> " It 's an important moment for the history of all teams . You see Germany and the heat they 're taking ( after being eliminated by Paraguay ) . Holland , the heat there ( after the seventh-ranked Dutch lost to sixth-ranked Morocco and their manager resigned ) . Everybody in the world has pressure on them right now , and what are you going to do with it ? Are you going to deliver ? Are you going to back away from it ? We 're going to try to attack it . " <p> Marsch admitted Canada was jittery to start the first match against Bosnia-Herzegovina in Toronto , and that there were nerves in the potential group clincher against Switzerland in Vancouver , too . He says South Africa 's speed was a challenge , since they closed down space . He said his first three matches as coach in 2024 -- Netherlands , France , Argentina ( two losses and a draw ) -- were intimidating , but set the bar he wanted to set . He said he hopes Morocco was worn down by 120 minutes in the Mexican heat against the Dutch . He says Morocco has no weaknesses , and yet : " Everyone 's going to expect us to lose , so let 's really go for it . Let 's really go for it . " <p> " ( Marsch 's personality ) matched very well with the group , " said midfielder Nathan Saliba . " I think he 's a genuine person ... and I think he found a group who 's ready to learn , ready to improve , and we saw results quickly . So it 's motivating to just keep believing ... now it 's all part of our DNA to be this way , to play like this , and to want to be better at doing it . " <p> On Saturday , Canada will need more than bravery and more than poise , and Jesse Marsch will have to hope he has equipped them with enough of everything to have a chance . He 'll find out . <p> Read more <p> Updated July 3 , 2026 at 4:11 p.m . <h> Canada is becoming a football nation . Now it needs a fight song <p> Canada needs a song to chant and sing to tie our football fans together . The question is , which one ? <p> Keith Birmingham Getty Images <p> Canada has reached the round of 16 at the World Cup , and will play Saturday against Morocco , in Houston . It could end there ; maybe it could continue . But when this run does conclude , what comes next ? How should football capture this momentum , and grow into the world 's game ? <p> We should learn to sing . <p> Between the watch parties , the TV numbers , the fan marches , and the joy in Toronto and Vancouver as so many Canadians and visiting fans lit up cities , this World Cup has felt like a turning point in Canada 's relationship with football . We are so far from a real footballing nation in so many ways , but one difference seems simple : many of the great football nations chant and sing , and Canada , broadly , does not . Or at least , not enough . <p> " I always thought we could have more fun , we could sing better songs , we could be more clever , the songs could be more related to what was going on on the pitch , " says Rob Notenboom , the president of the Voyageurs , Canada 's dominant football fan group . " We ( could ) get to have our own traditions . All these things would be very cool , and would add a cultural element that would just enrich us all , if that makes sense . " <p> This subject has been the subject of musings , searching , and barroom arguments among the Voyageurs for years . They have a page with the group 's favourite chants , and a handy songbook . The simplest is " Ol ? , Ol ? , Ol ? " but sung as " Allez , Allez , Allez . " When singing from whistle to whistle , simple is best . <p> Ten days after shattering his leg , Isma ? l Kon ? ditched his crutches and danced his way into Canada 's World Cup story . <p> But in football writ large there are also songs , which are usually identified with English football clubs . Often , they 're old , and sometimes nonsensical in any way other than tradition and participation . Getting Canadians to chant in unison is a hard one , even beyond the logistics . One time the Voyageurs gave out 1,500 chant sheets at a Canada game , and then it rained . <p> " People want that rhythmic English thing , but the thing is , when you go to U.K. , the songs are almost spontaneous , and they 're funny , " says Alex Ho , an executive director for the Voyageurs who is based in Ottawa . " And I do n't think we have that in our DNA . " He also points out that most Canadian fans do n't want to chant and sing all match . Not yet , anyway . <p> Europe and South America have legendary stadium anthems , but Canada is still searching for its signature chant . What should the Team Canada 's ' fight song ' be ? We asked Canada fans which iconic track needs to be heard across the pitch . <p> So what about a song to tie our football fans together ? Liverpool fans have been singing the most famous football song , " You 'll Never Walk Alone , " since the 1960s . Recent Premier League winners Arsenal adopted a song called " The Angel ( North London Forever ) , " which was written in 2022 . Manchester United sings John Denver 's " Take Me Home , Country Roads , " and West Ham sings " Forever Blowing Bubbles . " Sure . <h> From C ? line Dion to Great Big Sea , what song could unite us ? <p> We are mostly talking about singing the chorus , here , with bonus points if you can rewrite it with Canada-friendly phrases . You need something popular ; you need something catchy ; you 'd like something thematic . But then k.d. lang sang Leonard Cohen 's " Hallelujah " at the 2010 Olympics opening ceremony , and that song is almost entirely vibes . <p> This being Canada , the natural instinct is to look to boomer radio . " Life Is A Highway , " by Tom Cochrane ? Very singable , and perhaps the most popular Canadian song that could work for this . " Home For A Rest , " by Spirit of the West ? Very singable , and you could imagine rafts of Canadians in stadiums around the world bouncing in unison . Trooper 's " Raise A Little Hell " is very classic rock , but thematically strong and simple . " My Heart Will Go On , " by C ? line Dion ? Emotional , for sure . Think about it for a second , before throwing it away . <p> Ho mentioned " Ordinary Day , " by Great Big Sea ; it 's a rollicking , everyman semi-sea shanty that resonates with current Voyageurs . There is n't a Tragically Hip song that works , and newer Canadian stars like Justin Bieber , Drake , or The Weeknd do n't write anthemic singalongs that would be a hit with the nation 's football fans , do they ? <p> I called Max Kerman , the totemic lead singer of Arkells and an energetic Canadian treasure . He was reticent to offer a suggestion , including of his own band 's genuinely anthemic songs . <p> " The beautiful part about it , it 's like it is such a multicultural game , and so many of our stars are sons of immigrants and do n't care about Tom Cochrane , or do n't care about the Tragically Hip , " Kerman said . " The great thing with soccer is it feels like it 's been transported here by all the amazing immigrants that live in our country . So I 'm a little reticent to be like , ' Oh , we should do ' Summer of ' 69 , ' or something . " <p> His teammates call him " the heartbeat of this team . " His heroic goal will live on in the annals of Canadian sport . <h> The Voyageurs have been trying to crack this for years <p> There is something to that . In the Canada locker room after the South Africa win , Canadian players were dancing and singing along to a French hip-hop song called " Charger , " by Triangle des Bermudes . But for the record , Kerman 's favourite Canadian anthem at karaoke -- or in a concert , whichever -- is " Lost Together , " by Blue Rodeo . Which could work . <p> But , again , the Voyageurs have been debating this for years and , according to Ho and Notenboom , the challenges are legion . How do you build fan traditions when Canada does n't play enough in Canada ? The existing Voyageurs songbook came out of Toronto FC games , but there are different chants in Montreal and Vancouver , and finding a song that appeals to both anglophones and francophones is a challenge . ( C ? line Dion may be the obvious bridge here . ) Finding a song that appeals to both young and old is a challenge . ( " Life is a Highway " might -- might -- be the bridge here . ) The Voyageurs have tried the Kids In The Hall song , " These Are The Daves I Know , " which appeals to a relatively narrow slice of Canada . <p> And Canadian football fandom reflects not just the two solitudes of French and English , but also the mosaic of Canadian multiculturalism . Which , as Kerman noted , is a fundamental part of what makes Canada great . <p> " We 've got so many different cultures in this country , " Ho says . " The States has the same problem ( for football fandom ) . How do you connect all the different cultures ? <p> " Everybody 's got a different background , and soccer fans tend to be very pro-Canadian , but they also have this notion of what it is to be like . This goes fundamentally back to what does it mean to be a soccer football supporter ? " <p> It 's similar to the question of what makes us Canadian , and binds us together . Chants and songs reflected England , good and bad ; there were detours into racism , sexism , homophobia and more . You likely have to start with a popular song that appeals to an older crowd , and hope to pull everyone else along , and is somehow both organic and comes from the leaders in the Canadian fan movement . Tricky . <p> " It 's a situation where a critical mass of people have to kind of agree that 's a project that we need to work on , and are going to work on it , " says Notenboom , who is an amateur musician on the side and has written several songs for fan chants . " And people are going to participate . " <p> We should try . At its best the World Cup sells togetherness , pride in country and a shared humanity , and every piece of that is to the national good . It may be impossible ; we may be too Canadian , in so many ways . But if football is togetherness , why not try ? <p> Read more <p> SOCCER CITY <p> Your ultimate guide to the 2026 World Cup . @@138699079 <h> Weather Alert <p> Take action to protect yourself and others -- extreme heat can affect everyone 's health . Determine if you or others around you are at greater risk of heat illness . Check on older adults , those living alone and other at-risk people in-person or on the phone multiple times a day . Watch for the early signs of heat exhaustion in yourself and others . Signs may include headache , nausea , dizziness , thirst , dark urine and intense fatigue . Stop your activity and drink water . Heat stroke is a medical emergency ! Call 9-1-1 or your emergency health provider if you , or someone around you , is showing signs of heat stroke which can include red and hot skin , dizziness , nausea , confusion and change in consciousness . While you wait for medical attention , try to cool the person by moving them to a cool place , removing extra clothing , applying cold water or ice packs around the body . Drink water often and before you feel thirsty to replace fluids . Close blinds , or @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ . Turn on air conditioning , use a fan , or move to a cooler area of your living space . If your living space is hot , move to a cool public space such as a cooling centre , community centre , library or shaded park . Follow the advice of your region 's public health authority . Plan and schedule outdoor activities during the coolest parts of the day . Limit direct exposure to the sun and heat . Wear lightweight , light-coloured , loose-fitting clothing and a wide-brimmed hat . Never leave people , especially children , or pets inside a parked vehicle . Check the vehicle before locking to make sure no one is left behind . The long duration heat event is expected to end early this evening . What : Humidex values near the mid thirties . When : Continuing through this afternoon . @@138703003 <h> Carney to travel to Turkey , where NATO allies will focus on managing Trump <p> OTTAWA -- Prime Minister Mark Carney is scheduled to leave for Ankara on Monday to attend the annual NATO summit -- the first visit by a Canadian prime minister to Turkey since 2015 . <p> Turkey 's capital will make up the first leg of Carney 's week-long sojourn to the Middle East , which will conclude with a short stay in Saudi Arabia aimed at deepening trade and investment ties . <p> The July 7 and 8 NATO summit has been reduced in scale from the typical annual alliance gathering . What used to be two full days of meetings is now down to a dinner and one session where representatives of all member states will huddle . <p> Kerry Buck , Canada 's former ambassador to NATO , said this year 's gathering has been described as a " short , sweet summit to try avoid any drama , " given U.S. President Donald Trump 's propensity for disruption and insulting allies . <p> " This year 's meeting is , once @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ Trump committed to the alliance , " she said . <p> " Because he likes , almost , autocrats better than allies , the fact that it 's being hosted by Turkey is not a bad thing . He 's got less of an incentive to explode the thing . " <p> This NATO summit is taking place shortly after the recent G7 summit in France , which went smoothly . Allies will hope for the same this week as they discuss their ambitions for higher military spending . <p> NATO Secretary-General Mark Rutte has said member states must present " credible " plans to meet the new alliance commitment of spending five per cent of national GDP on defence by 2035 . <p> Canadian government officials told reporters in a background briefing on Friday that Carney will take part in a discussion on financing the defence sector and talk up Canada 's progress on boosting defence spending at the summit . <p> Carney 's schedule of bilateral meetings had yet to be finalized , the officials said . <p> The first part of Carney 's trip will also shine @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ , a NATO ally . <p> Turkish Foreign Minister Hakan Fidan has echoed the Canadian government 's argument that Europe should complement the military alliance without duplicating the work of NATO . <p> Chris Kilford , a former senior Canadian military attach ? in Turkey , said Ottawa can work on securing trade benefits from Turkey but progress could stall over geopolitical issues . <p> " There are many things that can test this relationship . But for now , we seem to be on a very positive track , and I think it 's to the benefit of both countries , " he said . <p> Kilford , who leads the Victoria branch of the Canadian International Council , said Canada and Turkey are both trying to diversify their trading partnerships to prevent supply chain shocks . <p> Turkey , for example , recently expressed interest in Canadian nuclear technology to reduce its dependence on nuclear projects involving Russia . <p> The Carney government has set out four pillars of its relationship with Turkey : energy exports , economic opportunities and trade , defence and security , and people-to-people @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ emerging market of broad interest to Canada , with opportunities for Canadian companies in agriculture , life sciences , mining and infrastructure . <p> Turkey has helped Ottawa evacuate Canadians from various Middle East crises and bring home activists detained by Israel for joining flotillas that sought to bring aid to Palestinians in Gaza . <p> Canada and Turkey have had a tricky relationship in recent years , particularly over matters concerning Canada 's large Armenian diaspora . Canada has long held that Turkey committed a genocide against Armenians a century ago , a claim that Ankara has repeatedly denied . <p> Ottawa also suspended arms sales to Turkey seven years ago over reports Turkey was diverting Canadian military components to Azerbaijan for use against Armenia and ethnic Armenians . Canada tightened those rules two years later , before dropping them . <p> Kilford said that resulted in Turkey 's " very lucrative " arms industry being cut off from Ontario-made surveillance and targeting sensors that had been installed in Turkish-made Bayraktar drones . <p> Instead , countries like Ukraine started buying the Canadian sensors and installing them in the @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ asylum to many Turkish political dissidents -- an uncommon move for countries that are military allies . <p> " As far as Turkey is concerned , though , we 're granting political asylum to terrorists . They do n't like it , " Kilford said , calling the country a flawed democracy . <p> Turkey has cancelled multiple LGBTQ+ events and the Committee to Protect Journalists says Turkey is among the top jailers of journalists . Kilford said the vast majority of Turkish media outlets are " either directly or indirectly owned by the government or government supporters . " <p> Ahead of the summit , Turkey has erected " miles and miles of barricades " across Ankara , Kilford said , which allows motorcades to cross the city with ease and makes street protests more difficult . <p> The country is currently gripped by high inflation , he said . Turkish President Recep Tayyip Erdogan has intervened in the country 's central bank , while many of his opponents have been jailed . <p> Kilford describes Turkey as an increasingly important state since it has maintained relative stability in @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ of Ukraine , the collapse of Syria 's dictatorship and the war Washington launched against Iran . <p> The country is touched by many major conflicts and by migration from Afghanistan and Syria . <p> Turkish Airlines , which Canada recently allowed to operate more flights , flies to hotspots most carriers wo n't or ca n't service , such as Kabul , Caracas and Mogadishu . <p> But Kilford said the Turkish government has long felt pushed to the periphery of geopolitical alliances like NATO and the European Union -- which it has been trying to join for years .

@@138749361 <p> There is no reason to think I 'd be discussing this , the latest in North American antisemitism , above all others , if it were not for its too-perfect overlap with another of my beats : the state of heterosexuality today . Or in this case , the absolute state of it . <p> A gay male politician , Democratic congressional candidate and California state senator Scott Wiener , was mobbed at a trans parade he says he 's attended every year since 2004 , accused of , among other things , not being " queer . " The ringleader ( though seemingly not the omnicauser who yelled , " You stopped being queer the moment you started supporting Israel , you piece of shit " ) , the self-appointed saviour of the queers , is one of those assigned-male-at-birth men who dates women . Straight dude , as they 're also known . An anti-YIMBY activist-poster , but sex coach by trade , Dimitry Yakoushkin was attending the parade in his capacities as an ally , in the derogatory sense . He was there to make the @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ of those are Palestinian suffering as versus opposition to high-rise apartment complexes I can not say . Just the guy to gatekeep queerness ! <p> Andrew Sullivan ( a pundit I once interned for ) , among others , flagged the absurdity . A gay man has lost his " queer " card , and at the behest of a hetero rabble-rouser . But the funniest part is the sort of straight person Yakoushkin is , or at least was in 2015 when he authored a Medium post about his Tinder usage : <p> " I 'm used to dating gay or queer women -- they do n't fit into the straight social paradigm , so they 're more likely to make decisions based on their desire rather than stick to a socially acceptable plan of not leading with sexuality . " <p> Queer , fine , this could mean bisexual . It could mean anything ! ( More on that in a moment ) . Good to know ! <p> In a video that Yakoushkin posted to Instagram , he begs for Wiener 's attention , pleading of him to Do Better on Gaza , while the band of omnicausers accompanying him chant , in the background , " We fucking hate you . " Yakoushkin , a prolific social media user , is adamant that he is no antisemite . It 's just that this one politician , who happens to be Jewish and pro-Palestine , and a Democrat , represents right-wing Zionism and rapacious landlords , or something . <p> Even just watching the video is a painful experience . Living it , I ca n't even imagine . If it were n't for Wiener 's stoic expression as the harassment continues ( he would later leave the event , however , because of it ) , it would veer into darkly hilarious . A free advertisement for the Republican party , which , for all its flaws , is not producing this . <p> Unsurprisingly , then , the line on the right -- or , to Wiener 's @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ progressives eating their own . Wiener had ( per Tablet ) done " everything right . He called Israel 's war against Hamas a ' genocide , ' rejected AIPAC , and supports cutting off U.S. military aid to Israel . None of it was enough. ? " <p> Batya Ungar-Sargon 's analysis includes the following accurate observation : " Being rabidly anti-Israel is the single most important litmus test on the Left . Without it , you can be as far Left as Scott Weiner sic on trans and gay issues and the Left will chase you out . But if you do hate Israel , you can be as far Right as Tucker Carlson on immigration and cultural issues and the Left will welcome you . " She also writes , " Weiner tried his best to appease this crowd on the Israel front . " <p> These interpretations get it partially right . The story demonstrates that , yes , even having the most impeccable positions on the previous current-thing ( trans rights ) as well as the new one ( Gaza ) does n't insulate a @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ trans journalist , points out on trans news website Assigned Media , the whole episode was pretty grim : <p> " When the ringleader of the harassment mob himself admits Wiener 's positions on Gaza are n't relevant to whether he deserves harassment , we can discount any quibbling about Wiener 's actual positions on Israel/Palestine and whether they go far enough ( for the record , I do n't believe a two state solution is still viable , which puts me to the left of Wiener ) . " <p> The first time I read that passage I thought Urquhart was , in the parenthetical , engaging in a spot of don't-be-mad-at-me . But it 's not quite that . It 's more that he 's emphasizing that it is in fact possible -- necessary , even -- to speak out against left antisemitism even when it 's directed at someone whichever key millimetres to the right of yourself . <p> Similarly -- and this is where I break from the Tablet and Ungar-Sargon interpretations -- I do n't think it 's accurate to look at someone like Wiener @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ 're stating their own positions , which are as far left as they seem . What reads to some as disingenuous , as ' please leave me alone , I 'm a good lefty ! ' is probably just what this man believes . <p> Certainly Wiener 's embrace of trans rights , in parades and legislation , is not a trendy affectation . For context of what it means for him to have been pro-trans in 2004 , I grew up in Manhattan , around plenty of openly gay people , and went to a high school with a gays-and-allies-type club . I had vague understandings of ' sex change ' as a thing , and understood it was not the same as crossdressing or drag . But it was only around 2006 , at the start of graduate school , that I even learned that transgender as a concept existed . <p> Worth noting that the assumption that Wiener must have changed his positions on Israel for political gain is shared by right-wing critics and by leftists who think he 's lying to win an election . In @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ mind in the face of new evidence is dismissed . <p> I ca n't read the man 's mind , but that Wiener 's position on whether to call Israeli actions in Gaza a " genocide " shifted could well be that his views have shifted along with events themselves , or at least events as filtered through his news sources . Regardless of where you fall on the question itself -- whether you think this is an offensive term to use in this context or whether you wish he 'd arrived there sooner -- what 's relevant here is that there 's nothing inherently suspicious about a position on an evolving story ... evolving . It 's certainly striking , as Joel Swanson points out , " that the assumption that Wiener must have changed his positions on Israel for political gain is shared by right-wing critics and by leftists who think he 's lying to win an election . In both cases , the possibility of genuinely changing one 's mind in the face of new evidence is dismissed . " There 's this strange consensus that inside @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ is a right-wing Israel supporter . <p> *** <p> In the New Yorker , in 2020 , M. Gessen called Democrat Pete Buttigieg " a straight politician in a gay man 's body . " In 2020 , that sort of assertion marked someone as a very with-it progressive . Gessen wrote that " people who grew up queer in the United States fall into two distinct categories of experience . One is the experience of never fitting in , being bullied by classmates for the way you walk , the way you look in clothes , the way you hit or fail to hit -- all the things that set you apart before you have language to describe them . " <p> That plenty of straight , cisgender children feel a bit different from the other kids and are bullied for perceived strangeness sheds some inadvertent light on the blurriness in current usages of " queer . " Rather than being about membership in a sexual or gender minority , queerness becomes the state of being more interesting than the sheeple masses . It gets decoupled -- as Kara @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ gender , and becomes its own axis of righteousness , one that includes a great many sexual-majority sorts while excluding any gay , bi , or trans people whose politics are n't radical enough , or who gravitate to khakis and monogamy . <p> I was reminded of the Gessen interlude when Buttigieg was recently separated overnight from his own young children in a swatting incident that has a distinct whiff of homophobia . Homophobia , as in , bigotry against gays and lesbians . Not against straight people who are simply so pro-Palestine they can not help but hassle queer Jewish politicians at queer events . Not against straight men who are simply so edgy that their type is women who do n't date men . <p> Where this leaves things politically is another matter . Wiener 's campaign saw a surge of donations , which is heartening . But it 's heartening in much the same way as Alfred Dreyfus getting exonerated was .
